# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>

}

    id : String

}

    id : String

    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}

    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer

    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}

    contractor : boolean
}

Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Sector::addCourse
Summary:
Adds a course to this sector and updates the course's sector reference accordingly.
Algorithm:
1. If course is null, return.
2. If course is already present in courses, do nothing.
3. If course belongs to another sector, remove it from that sector first.
4. Set course.sector to this sector.
5. Add course to courses if not already present.
Related Features:
- id
- courses
- addCourse(course : Course) : void
- removeCourse(course : Course) : void
Related Classes: Course
Input:
- course: the course to be added.
Output:
- None.
Pre-condition:
- The sector instance exists.
- course is not null.
Post-condition:
- The course belongs to this sector after completion.
- The course is present in courses at most once.
- If the course was previously in another sector, that previous association is removed.

Sector::removeCourse
Summary:
Removes a course from this sector and clears the course's sector reference if it points to this sector.
Algorithm:
1. If course is null, do nothing.
2. If courses does not contain course, do nothing.
3. Remove course from courses.
4. If course.sector == this sector, set course.sector to null.
Related Features:
- id
- courses
- addCourse(course : Course) : void
- removeCourse(course : Course) : void
Related Classes: Course
Input:
- course: the course to be removed.
Output:
- None.
Pre-condition:
- The sector instance exists.
- course is not null.
Post-condition:
- If course was present, it is no longer in courses.
- If the removed course referenced this sector, that reference is cleared.

# Operation Specifications
Course::addSession
Summary:
Adds a session to this course for the specified date, enforcing at most one session per course per day.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that date is not null and conforms to LocalDate semantics.
2. Search sessions for an existing session whose date equals the given date.
3. If such a session exists, do not create a new one; return the current sessions list.
4. Otherwise, create a new Session with the given date and no trainer/participants.
5. Append the new session to sessions.
6. Return the up-to-date sessions list.
Related Features:
- id
- sessions
- addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
- cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
Related Classes: Session
Input:
- date: the session date in LocalDate format; logically corresponds to yyyy-MM-dd.
- The date must be a valid calendar date.
Output:
- List<Session>: the current list of sessions of this course after the operation.
- The returned list includes the newly created session if insertion succeeds.
Pre-condition:
- The course instance exists.
- date is not null.
Post-condition:
- If no session existed on date, exactly one new session for that date is added.
- There is at most one session per course for any calendar day.
- The returned list reflects the current sessions state.

Course::addDocument
Summary:
Adds a referenced document to this course if it is not already associated with the course.
Algorithm:
1. If doc is null, return false.
2. Check whether documents already contains a document with the same name as doc.name.
3. If a document with the same name exists, return false.
4. Otherwise, append doc to documents.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
- id
- documents
- addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
- removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
Related Classes: Document
Input:
- doc: Document to be added.
- doc.name is the document name and should be non-empty.
- Document names are treated as the identity within one course.
Output:
- boolean: true if the document is successfully added; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The course instance exists.
- doc is not null.
- The caller intends to add a document reference to this course.
Post-condition:
- If the result is true, documents contains doc exactly once.
- If the result is false, documents is unchanged.

Course::cancelSession
Summary:
Cancels an existing session of this course on the specified date, but only if no participants are registered in that session.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that date is not null.
2. Find the session in sessions whose date equals the given date.
3. If no such session exists, return false.
4. If the session has any registered participants, return false.
5. Remove the session from sessions.
6. Return true.
Related Features:
- id
- sessions
- addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
- cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
Related Classes: Session, Participant
Input:
- date: the date of the session to cancel, in LocalDate format.
- The date must match an existing session date.
Output:
- boolean: true if the session is found and removed; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The course instance exists.
- date is not null.
- If the targeted session has participants, it is not eligible for cancellation.
Post-condition:
- If the result is true, the session for the given date is removed from sessions.
- If the result is false, sessions remains unchanged.
- Sessions with participants are preserved.

Course::removeDocument
Summary:
Removes a referenced document from this course if present.
Algorithm:
1. If doc is null, return false.
2. Search documents for a document matching doc by name.
3. If not found, return false.
4. Remove the matching document from documents.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
- id
- documents
- addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
- removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
Related Classes: Document
Input:
- doc: Document to be removed.
- doc.name is used as the matching key and should be non-empty.
Output:
- boolean: true if the document is removed; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The course instance exists.
- doc is not null.
Post-condition:
- If the result is true, documents no longer contains the matched document.
- If the result is false, documents is unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

```plantuml
    documents : List<Document>

    id : String

    removeCourse(course : Course) : void

    date : LocalDate

    contractor : boolean
```

# Operation Specifications
Course::addSession
Summary:
Adds a session to this course for the specified date, enforcing at most one session per course per day.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that date is not null and conforms to LocalDate semantics.
2. Search sessions for an existing session whose date equals the given date.
3. If such a session exists, do not create a new one; return the current sessions list.
4. Otherwise, create a new Session with the given date and no trainer/participants.
5. Append the new session to sessions.
6. Return the up-to-date sessions list.
Related Features:
- id
- sessions
- addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
- cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
Related Classes: Session
Input:
- date: the session date in LocalDate format; logically corresponds to yyyy-MM-dd.
- The date must be a valid calendar date.
Output:
- List<Session>: the current list of sessions of this course after the operation.
- The returned list includes the newly created session if insertion succeeds.
Pre-condition:
- The course instance exists.
- date is not null.
Post-condition:
- If no session existed on date, exactly one new session for that date is added.
- There is at most one session per course for any calendar day.
- The returned list reflects the current sessions state.

# Operation Specifications
Session::replaceTrainer
Summary:
Replaces the current trainer of this session with a new trainer, only if the operation is performed strictly before the session date.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that newTrainer and today are not null.
2. If today is not strictly earlier than date, return false.
3. Set trainer to newTrainer.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
- date
- trainer
- assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
- replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
Related Classes: Trainer
Input:
- newTrainer: trainer that will replace the current one.
- today: current date.
Output:
- boolean: true if replacement succeeds; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The session instance exists.
- newTrainer is not null.
- today is not null.
- today must be strictly earlier than date.
Post-condition:
- If true, trainer references newTrainer after the call.
- If false, trainer is unchanged.

Session::registerParticipant
Summary:
Registers a participant for this session if the participant is not already enrolled.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that p and today are not null.
2. If today is after the session date, return false only if business rules disallow late registration; however the requirement does not prohibit same-day registration, so do not reject based on date alone.
3. If registeredParticipants already contains p, return false.
4. Add p to registeredParticipants.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
- date
- registeredParticipants
- registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
- trainer
Related Classes: Participant
Input:
- p: participant to be registered.
- today: current date; included for consistency with the model, but no additional restriction is required by the requirement.
Output:
- boolean: true if the participant is added; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The session instance exists.
- p is not null.
- today is not null.
- The participant is not already registered for this session.
Post-condition:
- If true, registeredParticipants contains p exactly once.
- If false, registeredParticipants is unchanged.

Session::assignTrainer
Summary:
Assigns a trainer to this session only if the assignment happens strictly before the session date.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that t and today are not null.
2. If today is not strictly earlier than date, return false.
3. If trainer is already assigned, replace it only if this operation is intended as initial assignment; otherwise use replaceTrainer.
4. Set trainer to t.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
- date
- trainer
- assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
- replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
Related Classes: Trainer
Input:
- t: trainer to assign.
- today: current date.
- The operation is valid only when today < session date.
Output:
- boolean: true if the trainer is assigned; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The session instance exists.
- t is not null.
- today is not null.
- today must be strictly earlier than date.
Post-condition:
- If true, trainer references t after the call.
- If false, trainer is unchanged.

3. If trainer is already assigned, replace it only if this operation is intended as initial assignment; otherwise use replaceTrainer.
4. Set trainer to t.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
- date
- trainer
- assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
- replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
Related Classes: Trainer
Input:
- t: trainer to assign.
- today: current date.
- The operation is valid only when today < session date.
Output:
- boolean: true if the trainer is assigned; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The session instance exists.
- t is not null.
- today is not null.
- today must be strictly earlier than date.
Post-condition:
- If true, trainer references t after the call.
- If false, trainer is unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
School::addSector
Summary:
Adds a new sector to the school using a unique sector ID.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that id is not null or empty.
2. Search sectors for an existing sector whose id matches id.
3. If a match exists, return null or the existing sector? 
   Since the class diagram specifies return type Sector, infer that the operation returns the created sector on success and null on failure.
4. Otherwise create a new Sector with id.
5. Add the new sector to sectors.
6. Return the new sector.
Related Features:
- name
- courses
- sectors
- addSector(id : String) : Sector
Related Classes: Sector
Input:
- id: unique sector identifier.
- id must be non-empty.
Output:
- Sector: the newly created sector when successful; null when the sector already exists or input is invalid.
Pre-condition:
- The school instance exists.
- id is not null and not empty.
- No existing sector in the school has the same id.
Post-condition:
- If successful, sectors contains a new Sector with the given id.
- If unsuccessful, sectors is unchanged.

School::assignCourseToSector
Summary:
Assigns a course to a sector, ensuring the course belongs to exactly one sector at a time.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that courseId and sectorId are not null or empty.
2. Locate the course with id == courseId and the sector with id == sectorId.
3. If either is missing, return false.
4. If the course currently belongs to another sector, remove the course from that sector first.
5. Set the course's sector to the target sector.
6. Add the course to the target sector's course list if not already present.
7. Return true.
Related Features:
- courses
- sectors
- addCourse(id : String) : boolean
- addSector(id : String) : Sector
- assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
Related Classes: Course, Sector
Input:
- courseId: unique ID of the course to assign.
- sectorId: unique ID of the sector to receive the course.
Output:
- boolean: true if assignment succeeds; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The school instance exists.
- A course with courseId exists.
- A sector with sectorId exists.
- The identifiers are not null or empty.
Post-condition:
- If true, the course is removed from any previous sector and added to the target sector.
- The course.sector reference points to the target sector.
- The course appears in exactly one sector's course list, namely the target sector.
- If false, no associations are changed.

School::addCourse
Summary:
Adds a new course to the school using a case-sensitive unique course ID.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that id is not null or empty.
2. Search courses for an existing course whose id exactly matches id.
3. If a match exists, return false.
4. Otherwise create a new Course with id.
5. Add the new course to courses.
6. Return true.
Related Features:
- name
- courses
- sectors
- addCourse(id : String) : boolean
- addSector(id : String) : Sector
- assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
Related Classes: Course
Input:
- id: unique course identifier.
- The comparison is case-sensitive.
- id must be non-empty.
Output:
- boolean: true if the course is created and added; otherwise false.
Pre-condition:
- The school instance exists.
- id is not null and not empty.
- No existing course in the school has the same id with identical case.
Post-condition:
- If true, courses contains exactly one new Course with the given id.
- If false, courses is unchanged.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Adds a session to this course for the specified date, enforcing at most one session per course per day.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Validate that date is not null and conforms to LocalDate semantics.
   * 2. Search sessions for an existing session whose date equals the given date.
   * 3. If such a session exists, do not create a new one; return the current sessions list.
   * 4. Otherwise, create a new Session with the given date and no trainer/participants.
   * 5. Append the new session to sessions.
   * 6. Return the up-to-date sessions list.
   * Related Features:
   * - id
   * - sessions
   * - addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
   * - cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
   * Related Classes: Session
   * Input:
   * - date: the session date in LocalDate format; logically corresponds to yyyy-MM-dd.
   * - The date must be a valid calendar date.
   * Output:
   * - List<Session>: the current list of sessions of this course after the operation.
   * - The returned list includes the newly created session if insertion succeeds.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The course instance exists.
   * - date is not null.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If no session existed on date, exactly one new session for that date is added.
   * - There is at most one session per course for any calendar day.
   * - The returned list reflects the current sessions state.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model dateDataType="edu.trainingSchool.LocalDate" dateRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Session> addSession(final LocalDate date)
  {
    if (date == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("date must not be null");
    }

    EList<Session> sessions = getSessions();
    for (Session session : sessions)
    {
      if (date.equals(session.getDate()))
      {
        return sessions;
      }
    }

    Session newSession = TrainingSchoolFactory.eINSTANCE.createSession();
    newSession.setDate(date);
    sessions.add(newSession);
    return sessions;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Cancels an existing session of this course on the specified date, but only if no participants are registered in that session.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Validate that date is not null.
   * 2. Find the session in sessions whose date equals the given date.
   * 3. If no such session exists, return false.
   * 4. If the session has any registered participants, return false.
   * 5. Remove the session from sessions.
   * 6. Return true.
   * Related Features:

   * - sessions
   * - addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
   * - cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean

   * - The date must match an existing session date.
   * Output:
   * - boolean: true if the session is found and removed; otherwise false.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The course instance exists.
   * - date is not null.
   * - If the targeted session has participants, it is not eligible for cancellation.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If the result is true, the session for the given date is removed from sessions.
   * - If the result is false, sessions remains unchanged.
   * - Sessions with participants are preserved.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model dateDataType="edu.trainingSchool.LocalDate" dateRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean cancelSession(final LocalDate date)
  {
    if (date == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("date must not be null");
    }

    EList<Session> sessions = getSessions();
    for (Session session : sessions)
    {
      if (date.equals(session.getDate()))
      {
        if (session.getRegisteredParticipants() != null && !session.getRegisteredParticipants().isEmpty())
        {
          return false;
        }
        sessions.remove(session);
        return true;
      }
    }
    return false;
    
  }

   * 1. If doc is null, return false.
   * 2. Check whether documents already contains a document with the same name as doc.name.

   * - doc is not null.

   * Post-condition:
   * - If the result is true, documents contains doc exactly once.
   * - If the result is false, documents is unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model docRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean addDocument(final Document doc)
  {
    if (doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }

    EList<Document> documents = getDocuments();
    String name = doc.getName();
    for (Document existing : documents)
    {
      if (name == null ? existing.getName() == null : name.equals(existing.getName()))
      {
        return false;
      }
    }

    documents.add(doc);
    return true;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Removes a referenced document from this course if present.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If doc is null, return false.
   * 2. Search documents for a document matching doc by name.
   * 3. If not found, return false.
   * 4. Remove the matching document from documents.
   * 5. Return true.
   * Related Features:
   * - id
   * - documents
   * - addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
   * - removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
   * Related Classes: Document
   * Input:
   * - doc: Document to be removed.
   * - doc.name is used as the matching key and should be non-empty.
   * Output:
   * - boolean: true if the document is removed; otherwise false.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The course instance exists.
   * - doc is not null.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If the result is true, documents no longer contains the matched document.
   * - If the result is false, documents is unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model docRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean removeDocument(final Document doc)
  {
    if (doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }

    EList<Document> documents = getDocuments();
    String name = doc.getName();
    for (int i = 0; i < documents.size(); i++)
    {
      Document existing = documents.get(i);
      if (name == null ? existing.getName() == null : name.equals(existing.getName()))
      {
        documents.remove(i);
        return true;
      }
    }
    return false;
    
  }

  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
